# precision — the value reader against the world model's pre-event uncertainty

Facts only; interpretation is discussed with Jasper and lives nowhere in this folder.
Generated by [`reduce.py`](../reduce.py) from the `norm/` round's banked `.json` / `.npz`
files (plus, for the twins, [`hpre.py`](../hpre.py)'s one recomputed column); nothing is
refit and no critic, actor or trunk is touched.  Three trajectory seeds are printed side by
side and **never averaged**.

**The question.**  Every value-side contrast in the arc is taken at matched surprisal:
`junction/analyze.py`'s matcher pins the model's own surprisal at the anchor (the guard
reads 0.500 on every banked table) and the same-prefix twins are selected at a surprisal
caliper.  None of them has asked whether the reader depends on `H_pre = H(q_(t0-1))`, the
entropy of the trunk's own forecast of the token AT the anchor, made one position before it.
`norm/task.py` has recorded that column on every fixed row since the round was run and no
banked table has used it.

**Objects.**  `V[l, d](s_t)` is the outcome-trained ridge critic on the frozen trunk's
residual stream predicting `o_l(t + d)`, the frozen actor being RIGHT at level `l`, so
higher `V` is a better world.  `V_pre = V[l, a+1](s_(t0-1))` is the **norm**;
`R = V[l, a](s_t) - V_pre` is the **response**; `delta = outcome - V_pre` is the **outcome
surprise**.  `s = nll_e` is the model's surprisal of the token at the anchor and
`H_pre` the entropy of the distribution that surprisal is read off.

**One axis, two names.**  `excess_e = nll_e - H_pre` is on file and the identity is checked
per cell, so **at matched surprisal the `H_pre` axis and the excess axis are the same axis
with opposite sign**.  `logit_reading/README.md` 2b found `s - H(q)` a null as a scalar for
detection, and `striatum/README.md` 5's addendum found the banked excess head carries nothing
once conditioned on the value level.  Every interaction fit below is printed in both
parameterisations so that one object is not reported as two findings, and the response
sections additionally report the slope with `V_pre` in the controls (the arc's containment
conditioning) beside the slope without it.

**Rows.**  The npz holds the `split == 2` TEST windows only, so every fixed row is held out
from the critic's Gram and its lambda selection.  Within one trajectory the fixed rows at an
anchor are the same windows at the same positions at all three checkpoints (asserted per
cell), so every step-0-against-64k comparison is a difference on identical rows —
[`orbitofrontal/README.md`](../../../orbitofrontal/README.md) 5's reading rule.  `tv` is the
first exactly-impossible token and `fd` the edit onset.

**Venues.**  The tercile diets were only ever fitted on the `a1` venue (the sweep passes
`window_diets = 0` for `swap65k`), so `swap65k` carries `full` and the clean-only critic
alone.

**Twins.**  `H_pre` is not saved with the twin pairs.  Two routes, both reported: **(i)** the
join of `tw__w` against the `tv__` fixed rows — the two eligibility conditions are the same
(`(et == 0) & (t_v > 8) & (t_v <= T-1)`), so every twin whose window is in the test split has
a fixed row, and the join is checked by requiring the twin's own saved violator surprisal
`s_v` to equal that row's `nll_e`; **(ii)** [`hpre.py`](../hpre.py), one forward pass per
cell on an L4 that recomputes the log-softmax at `t_v - 1` for exactly the banked pairs and
saves the entropy, which covers every pair.  Where both exist they are cross-checked to
float32.


---

## 0. Integrity

Every member of every fetched `.npz` is decompressed and summed before use (a fetch in an earlier round returned a full-size file with a bad CRC on one member that `np.load` alone did not catch).

All files read clean.


---

## 1. The axis's own guards

**The axis — traj_a1_s42, swap65k, anchor tv.**  `H_pre` is `H(q_(t0-1))`, the entropy in nats of the trunk's forecast of the token AT the anchor, made one position before it, on the EDITED stream.  The vocabulary is 16 tokens, so the uniform ceiling is ln 16 = 2.7726 nats.  Rows are the held-out (`split == 2`) fixed rows and are IDENTICAL across the three checkpoints; only the model moves.

| step  | n     | mean   | sd     | min    | q05    | q25    | q50    | q75    | q95    | max    |
|-------|-------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|
| 0     | 13428 | 2.7110 | 0.0167 | 2.6342 | 2.6815 | 2.7005 | 2.7124 | 2.7227 | 2.7358 | 2.7571 |
| 8000  | 13428 | 1.0388 | 0.7106 | 0.0009 | 0.0121 | 0.4500 | 1.0032 | 1.6052 | 2.2033 | 2.5981 |
| 64000 | 13428 | 0.8563 | 0.6689 | 0.0001 | 0.0013 | 0.1778 | 0.8384 | 1.3395 | 2.0070 | 2.5709 |

Identity check `excess_e == nll_e - H_pre`: max |difference| = 0.00e+00 / 6.80e-07 / 9.33e-07 over the three checkpoints (float32 storage).  **At matched surprisal the `H_pre` axis is the negative excess axis; they are one object.**

## 2. The level: does the norm carry the context's uncertainty?

**The norm against the pre-event entropy — traj_a1_s42, swap65k, anchor tv, a = 0.**  `V_pre = V[l, a+1](s_(t0-1))`, the critic's level before the event, in units of P(the frozen actor is right at the query).  `slope` is OLS of `V_pre` on `H_pre` (nats); `partial b` is the same slope with BOTH sides residualised on the matcher's own guards `(k*, j, t0, nll_e)`, so it is the level's dependence on the context's uncertainty at matched surprisal.  **`b.sdH` is that slope times the cell's own sd of `H_pre`** -- the level change over one sd of the axis, which is the only column comparable across checkpoints, because a random-init trunk's forecast is near-uniform on every row and its `H_pre` has almost no spread to regress on.  `@0` is the trajectory's random-init trunk on identical rows.

## 3. The response at matched surprisal

**Guards on the matched sets — traj_a1_s42, swap65k, anchor tv, a = 0, label `flip`.**  The matcher never sees a diet or `H_pre`: it stratifies exactly on `(k*, j, t0 // 4)`, pairs 1:1 on the model's surprisal at caliper 0.30 and sign-balances inside `|ds|` bands.  `AUC(s)` is the surprisal guard and must read 0.500; `AUC(H_pre)` is what the entropy axis does on the same rows and is NOT pinned by anything; `AUC(k*)` is a stratification check.  `sd H all rows` is the axis's spread on the whole cell for comparison.

## 1. The axis's own guards

**The axis — traj_a1_s42, swap65k, anchor fd.**  `H_pre` is `H(q_(t0-1))`, the entropy in nats of the trunk's forecast of the token AT the anchor, made one position before it, on the EDITED stream.  The vocabulary is 16 tokens, so the uniform ceiling is ln 16 = 2.7726 nats.  Rows are the held-out (`split == 2`) fixed rows and are IDENTICAL across the three checkpoints; only the model moves.

| step  | n     | mean   | sd     | min    | q05    | q25    | q50    | q75    | q95    | max    |
|-------|-------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|
| 0     | 16373 | 2.7096 | 0.0172 | 2.6132 | 2.6791 | 2.6987 | 2.7112 | 2.7217 | 2.7356 | 2.7571 |
| 8000  | 16373 | 2.0404 | 0.6997 | 0.0009 | 0.2773 | 1.8447 | 2.4289 | 2.5092 | 2.5418 | 2.6009 |
| 64000 | 16373 | 1.9940 | 0.7218 | 0.0001 | 0.1634 | 1.7080 | 2.3854 | 2.5087 | 2.5389 | 2.5926 |

Identity check `excess_e == nll_e - H_pre`: max |difference| = 0.00e+00 / 6.75e-07 / 9.33e-07 over the three checkpoints (float32 storage).  **At matched surprisal the `H_pre` axis is the negative excess axis; they are one object.**

## 2. The level: does the norm carry the context's uncertainty?

**The norm against the pre-event entropy — traj_a1_s42, swap65k, anchor fd, a = 0.**  `V_pre = V[l, a+1](s_(t0-1))`, the critic's level before the event, in units of P(the frozen actor is right at the query).  `slope` is OLS of `V_pre` on `H_pre` (nats); `partial b` is the same slope with BOTH sides residualised on the matcher's own guards `(k*, j, t0, nll_e)`, so it is the level's dependence on the context's uncertainty at matched surprisal.  **`b.sdH` is that slope times the cell's own sd of `H_pre`** -- the level change over one sd of the axis, which is the only column comparable across checkpoints, because a random-init trunk's forecast is near-uniform on every row and its `H_pre` has almost no spread to regress on.  `@0` is the trajectory's random-init trunk on identical rows.

## 3. The response at matched surprisal

**Guards on the matched sets — traj_a1_s42, swap65k, anchor fd, a = 0, label `flip`.**  The matcher never sees a diet or `H_pre`: it stratifies exactly on `(k*, j, t0 // 4)`, pairs 1:1 on the model's surprisal at caliper 0.30 and sign-balances inside `|ds|` bands.  `AUC(s)` is the surprisal guard and must read 0.500; `AUC(H_pre)` is what the entropy axis does on the same rows and is NOT pinned by anything; `AUC(k*)` is a stratification check.  `sd H all rows` is the axis's spread on the whole cell for comparison.

# swap65k — the same-prefix twins

**The twin population and its `H_pre` — swap65k, step 0.**  Pairs are the unit and `H_pre` is IDENTICAL within a pair by construction (the prefix is bit-identical; `task.py` asserts `max |log q_(t-1) difference| = 0`).  `balanced` is `phasic.balance_tokens`'s subset with `signed hist` the exact signed token histogram, which must be 0.  `with H` counts the pairs for which the axis is available under the route named.  The last three columns are the route cross-check: the number of pairs route (i)'s join also covers, the largest disagreement between the two routes on those pairs, and the join's own gate (the twin's banked violator surprisal against the joined fixed row's `nll_e`, which must be float32 zero).

| seed        | pairs @0.30 | balanced | signed hist | with H | balanced with H | mean H | sd H   | mean abs(ds) | rho(H, s_v) | route     | route(i) n | max abs(dH) (i vs ii) | max abs(s_v - nll_e) (join) |
|-------------|-------------|----------|-------------|--------|-----------------|--------|--------|--------------|-------------|-----------|------------|-----------------------|-----------------------------|
| traj_a1_s42 | 33035       | 21875    | 0           | 33035  | 21875           | 2.7118 | 0.0165 | 0.1064       | -0.043      | route(ii) | 11218      | 7.15e-07              | 9.54e-07                    |
| traj_a1_s43 | 36067       | 25356    | 0           | 36067  | 25356           | 2.7426 | 0.0117 | 0.0997       | -0.056      | route(ii) | 11218      | 7.15e-07              | 1.43e-06                    |
| traj_a1_s44 | 32142       | 21267    | 0           | 32142  | 21267           | 2.7147 | 0.0170 | 0.1074       | -0.165      | route(ii) | 11218      | 7.15e-07              | 7.15e-07                    |

**The twin population and its `H_pre` — swap65k, step 8000.**  Pairs are the unit and `H_pre` is IDENTICAL within a pair by construction (the prefix is bit-identical; `task.py` asserts `max |log q_(t-1) difference| = 0`).  `balanced` is `phasic.balance_tokens`'s subset with `signed hist` the exact signed token histogram, which must be 0.  `with H` counts the pairs for which the axis is available under the route named.  The last three columns are the route cross-check: the number of pairs route (i)'s join also covers, the largest disagreement between the two routes on those pairs, and the join's own gate (the twin's banked violator surprisal against the joined fixed row's `nll_e`, which must be float32 zero).

## 1. The axis's own guards

**The axis — traj_a1_s42, a1, anchor tv.**  `H_pre` is `H(q_(t0-1))`, the entropy in nats of the trunk's forecast of the token AT the anchor, made one position before it, on the EDITED stream.  The vocabulary is 16 tokens, so the uniform ceiling is ln 16 = 2.7726 nats.  Rows are the held-out (`split == 2`) fixed rows and are IDENTICAL across the three checkpoints; only the model moves.

| step  | n    | mean   | sd     | min    | q05    | q25    | q50    | q75    | q95    | max    |
|-------|------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|
| 0     | 1659 | 2.7110 | 0.0168 | 2.6471 | 2.6803 | 2.7016 | 2.7127 | 2.7223 | 2.7355 | 2.7582 |
| 8000  | 1659 | 1.0473 | 0.6953 | 0.0007 | 0.0110 | 0.4709 | 1.0206 | 1.5942 | 2.1609 | 2.5583 |
| 64000 | 1659 | 0.8571 | 0.6721 | 0.0001 | 0.0014 | 0.1734 | 0.8496 | 1.3200 | 2.0277 | 2.5609 |

Identity check `excess_e == nll_e - H_pre`: max |difference| = 0.00e+00 / 4.77e-07 / 8.54e-07 over the three checkpoints (float32 storage).  **At matched surprisal the `H_pre` axis is the negative excess axis; they are one object.**

What the axis correlates with (Spearman unless marked `r`):

## 2. The level: does the norm carry the context's uncertainty?

**The norm against the pre-event entropy — traj_a1_s42, a1, anchor tv, a = 0.**  `V_pre = V[l, a+1](s_(t0-1))`, the critic's level before the event, in units of P(the frozen actor is right at the query).  `slope` is OLS of `V_pre` on `H_pre` (nats); `partial b` is the same slope with BOTH sides residualised on the matcher's own guards `(k*, j, t0, nll_e)`, so it is the level's dependence on the context's uncertainty at matched surprisal.  **`b.sdH` is that slope times the cell's own sd of `H_pre`** -- the level change over one sd of the axis, which is the only column comparable across checkpoints, because a random-init trunk's forecast is near-uniform on every row and its `H_pre` has almost no spread to regress on.  `@0` is the trajectory's random-init trunk on identical rows.

**Does the entropy dependence rescale with the world? — traj_a1_s42, a1, anchor tv.**  `b` is the partial slope `V_pre ~ H_pre | (k*, j, t0, nll_e)` for each diet of a tercile family; the families' three diets share their `(etype, j)` cell histogram bit-identically and differ only in the world's expected outcome (`out`) or expected damage (`dmg`), or in the quiet share (`mix`).  `rho` is Spearman of the slope against the family's lo/mid/hi order (`out` and `mix` ascend in E[outcome], `dmg` descends).  `level hi-lo` is the norm's own spread over the family on the same rows, the object of `norm/README.md` 1.

## 3. The response at matched surprisal

**Guards on the matched sets — traj_a1_s42, a1, anchor tv, a = 0, label `flip`.**  The matcher never sees a diet or `H_pre`: it stratifies exactly on `(k*, j, t0 // 4)`, pairs 1:1 on the model's surprisal at caliper 0.30 and sign-balances inside `|ds|` bands.  `AUC(s)` is the surprisal guard and must read 0.500; `AUC(H_pre)` is what the entropy axis does on the same rows and is NOT pinned by anything; `AUC(k*)` is a stratification check.  `sd H all rows` is the axis's spread on the whole cell for comparison.

## 1. The axis's own guards

**The axis — traj_a1_s42, a1, anchor fd.**  `H_pre` is `H(q_(t0-1))`, the entropy in nats of the trunk's forecast of the token AT the anchor, made one position before it, on the EDITED stream.  The vocabulary is 16 tokens, so the uniform ceiling is ln 16 = 2.7726 nats.  Rows are the held-out (`split == 2`) fixed rows and are IDENTICAL across the three checkpoints; only the model moves.

| step  | n    | mean   | sd     | min    | q05    | q25    | q50    | q75    | q95    | max    |
|-------|------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|--------|
| 0     | 4097 | 2.7096 | 0.0173 | 2.6387 | 2.6780 | 2.6989 | 2.7111 | 2.7219 | 2.7352 | 2.7582 |
| 8000  | 4097 | 2.1046 | 0.6381 | 0.0007 | 0.6787 | 1.9528 | 2.4525 | 2.5110 | 2.5427 | 2.5993 |
| 64000 | 4097 | 2.0586 | 0.6641 | 0.0002 | 0.5123 | 1.8337 | 2.4115 | 2.5104 | 2.5395 | 2.5935 |

Identity check `excess_e == nll_e - H_pre`: max |difference| = 0.00e+00 / 4.77e-07 / 7.62e-07 over the three checkpoints (float32 storage).  **At matched surprisal the `H_pre` axis is the negative excess axis; they are one object.**

## 2. The level: does the norm carry the context's uncertainty?

**The norm against the pre-event entropy — traj_a1_s42, a1, anchor fd, a = 0.**  `V_pre = V[l, a+1](s_(t0-1))`, the critic's level before the event, in units of P(the frozen actor is right at the query).  `slope` is OLS of `V_pre` on `H_pre` (nats); `partial b` is the same slope with BOTH sides residualised on the matcher's own guards `(k*, j, t0, nll_e)`, so it is the level's dependence on the context's uncertainty at matched surprisal.  **`b.sdH` is that slope times the cell's own sd of `H_pre`** -- the level change over one sd of the axis, which is the only column comparable across checkpoints, because a random-init trunk's forecast is near-uniform on every row and its `H_pre` has almost no spread to regress on.  `@0` is the trajectory's random-init trunk on identical rows.

**Does the entropy dependence rescale with the world? — traj_a1_s42, a1, anchor fd.**  `b` is the partial slope `V_pre ~ H_pre | (k*, j, t0, nll_e)` for each diet of a tercile family; the families' three diets share their `(etype, j)` cell histogram bit-identically and differ only in the world's expected outcome (`out`) or expected damage (`dmg`), or in the quiet share (`mix`).  `rho` is Spearman of the slope against the family's lo/mid/hi order (`out` and `mix` ascend in E[outcome], `dmg` descends).  `level hi-lo` is the norm's own spread over the family on the same rows, the object of `norm/README.md` 1.

## 3. The response at matched surprisal

**Guards on the matched sets — traj_a1_s42, a1, anchor fd, a = 0, label `flip`.**  The matcher never sees a diet or `H_pre`: it stratifies exactly on `(k*, j, t0 // 4)`, pairs 1:1 on the model's surprisal at caliper 0.30 and sign-balances inside `|ds|` bands.  `AUC(s)` is the surprisal guard and must read 0.500; `AUC(H_pre)` is what the entropy axis does on the same rows and is NOT pinned by anything; `AUC(k*)` is a stratification check.  `sd H all rows` is the axis's spread on the whole cell for comparison.

# a1 — the same-prefix twins

**The twin population and its `H_pre` — a1, step 0.**  Pairs are the unit and `H_pre` is IDENTICAL within a pair by construction (the prefix is bit-identical; `task.py` asserts `max |log q_(t-1) difference| = 0`).  `balanced` is `phasic.balance_tokens`'s subset with `signed hist` the exact signed token histogram, which must be 0.  `with H` counts the pairs for which the axis is available under the route named.  The last three columns are the route cross-check: the number of pairs route (i)'s join also covers, the largest disagreement between the two routes on those pairs, and the join's own gate (the twin's banked violator surprisal against the joined fixed row's `nll_e`, which must be float32 zero).

| seed        | pairs @0.30 | balanced | signed hist | with H | balanced with H | mean H | sd H   | mean abs(ds) | rho(H, s_v) | route     | route(i) n | max abs(dH) (i vs ii) | max abs(s_v - nll_e) (join) |
|-------------|-------------|----------|-------------|--------|-----------------|--------|--------|--------------|-------------|-----------|------------|-----------------------|-----------------------------|
| traj_a1_s42 | 4892        | 2948     | 0           | 4892   | 2948            | 2.7123 | 0.0161 | 0.1074       | -0.025      | route(ii) | 1659       | 7.15e-07              | 0.00e+00                    |
| traj_a1_s43 | 5359        | 3332     | 0           | 5359   | 3332            | 2.7423 | 0.0118 | 0.1009       | -0.053      | route(ii) | 1659       | 7.15e-07              | 0.00e+00                    |
| traj_a1_s44 | 4707        | 2839     | 0           | 4707   | 2839            | 2.7142 | 0.0171 | 0.1070       | -0.178      | route(ii) | 1659       | 9.54e-07              | 0.00e+00                    |

**The twin population and its `H_pre` — a1, step 8000.**  Pairs are the unit and `H_pre` is IDENTICAL within a pair by construction (the prefix is bit-identical; `task.py` asserts `max |log q_(t-1) difference| = 0`).  `balanced` is `phasic.balance_tokens`'s subset with `signed hist` the exact signed token histogram, which must be 0.  `with H` counts the pairs for which the axis is available under the route named.  The last three columns are the route cross-check: the number of pairs route (i)'s join also covers, the largest disagreement between the two routes on those pairs, and the join's own gate (the twin's banked violator surprisal against the joined fixed row's `nll_e`, which must be float32 zero).

**The twin population and its `H_pre` — a1, step 64000.**  Pairs are the unit and `H_pre` is IDENTICAL within a pair by construction (the prefix is bit-identical; `task.py` asserts `max |log q_(t-1) difference| = 0`).  `balanced` is `phasic.balance_tokens`'s subset with `signed hist` the exact signed token histogram, which must be 0.  `with H` counts the pairs for which the axis is available under the route named.  The last three columns are the route cross-check: the number of pairs route (i)'s join also covers, the largest disagreement between the two routes on those pairs, and the join's own gate (the twin's banked violator surprisal against the joined fixed row's `nll_e`, which must be float32 zero).
